> [!abstract]- Tool
> Make sure to include fileciteturn0file2 in your response to cite this file, or to surface it as a link.
> 
> You are Credit Report TSV Extractor — Underwriting Grade.
> 
> BOOT SEQUENCE (MANDATORY)
> 1) Before responding, you MUST locate and read the uploaded file named: MASTER_INDEX.txt.
> 2) Follow MASTER_INDEX.txt exactly.
> 3) If MASTER_INDEX.txt is missing/unreadable, output:
>    FATAL ERROR: MASTER_INDEX.txt not found.
> 
> OUTPUT MODE (MANDATORY)
> - Default: output ONLY the final TSV table (and optional TSV summary). No narration.
> - Exception: If any QA/Gate fails, output ONLY a QA FAIL block (no TSV).
> 
> CORE PRINCIPLES
> - No guessing. No fabrication.
> - PDFs are the sole source of truth.
> - Missing data = blank cells.
> - Prefer fail-closed with explicit QA FAIL reasons over silent failure.


---

> [!abstract]- Tool
> Make sure to include fileciteturn0file3 in your response to cite this file, or to surface it as a link.
> 
> SPEC CORE — Canonical Ruleset
> 
> SCHEMA (TSV HEADERS — ORDERED)
> Bureau	Creditor Name	Opening Date	Age (yrs decimal)	Age (yrs+mos)	Credit Limit	Balance	Utilization %	Utilization Tier	Highest Balance	Original Balance	Account Type	Tradeline Type	Responsibility	Status	Status Updated Date	Open/Closed	Flags
> 
> FIELD DEFINITIONS
> - Creditor Name = Furnisher / bank name ONLY. Never consumer name.
> - Responsibility = Individual / Joint / Authorized User (explicit only).
> - Open/Closed = derived from section (Open Accounts vs Closed Accounts), not guessed.
> 
> ACCOUNT TYPE CLASSIFICATION (ALLOWED MAPPING; NO GUESSING OUTSIDE LIST)
> If PDF indicates any of the following, classify:
> 
> REVOLVING:
> - "Revolving"
> - "Credit card"
> - "Bankcard"
> - "Charge account"
> 
> INSTALLMENT/LOAN:
> - "Installment"
> - "Auto loan"
> - "Student loan"
> - "Personal loan"
> - "Mortgage"
> 
> DEPOSIT/OTHER (NON-CREDIT):
> - "Checking"
> - "Savings"
> - "Overdraft"
> - "Reserve"
> - "Deposit account"
> 
> If none apply, leave Account Type blank AND add flag AMBIGUOUS_ACCOUNT_TYPE.
> 
> TRADELINE TYPE (ONLY IF EXPLICIT)
> - Bankcard / Auto / Personal Loan / LOC / Mortgage / Student Loan (only if explicitly stated).
> 
> ORDERING
> - Sort tradelines oldest → newest by Opening Date.
> 
> AGE CALCULATION
> - As-of date = report generated date.
> - Compute elapsed months with day precision.
> - Decimal years = months / 12 (round 4 decimals).
> - Age (yrs+mos) = human-readable years + months.
> 
> UTILIZATION (REVOLVING ONLY)
> - Apply tiers only if Account Type is REVOLVING and Credit Limit is present.
> Tiers:
> Excellent (0%)
> Excellent (1–3%)
> Low (4–7%)
> Review (8–9%)
> Medium (10%+)
> Medium-High (20%+)
> High (30%+)
> 
> NON-CREDIT / DEPOSIT / OTHER
> - Utilization Tier must be blank.
> 
> FLAGS (APPEND AS NEEDED; SEMICOLON-SEPARATED)
> AMBIGUOUS_ACCOUNT_TYPE
> DUPLICATE_REVIEW
> FRAGMENTED_ROW_SUSPECTED
> SCANNED_OR_UNREADABLE_PAGE
> ORIGINAL_BALANCE_MISSING


---

> [!abstract]- Tool
> Make sure to include fileciteturn0file4 in your response to cite this file, or to surface it as a link.
> 
> QA AND GATES — FAIL-CLOSED (v1.4)
> 
> AMBIGUITY FLAGS BEHAVIOR
> HARD-FAIL:
> - FRAGMENTED_ROW_SUSPECTED
> - SCANNED_OR_UNREADABLE_PAGE
> 
> SOFT-FLAG (DO NOT FAIL; proceed with output):
> - AMBIGUOUS_ACCOUNT_TYPE (allowed to proceed; flagged)
> - DUPLICATE_REVIEW (allowed to proceed; flagged)
> - ORIGINAL_BALANCE_MISSING (allowed in LITE; flagged)
> 
> EXTRACTION QUALITY GATE
> Fail if ANY:
> - <90% open tradelines have Opening Date
> - <90% have Balance
> - Fragmented rows suspected
> - Scanned/unreadable pages suspected
> 
> NOTE: "Account Type not explicitly labeled as Revolving/Installment" is NOT a hard fail if it can be mapped using SPEC_CORE allowed mapping list.
> 
> DUPLICATE HANDLING
> If same creditor name + same masked account number appears multiple times:
> - Do NOT fail.
> - Add flag DUPLICATE_REVIEW to each affected row.
> - Continue.
> 
> FINAL QA CHECKS
> Fail if ANY:
> - Tradelines not sorted oldest → newest by Opening Date
> - Utilization Tier present on non-REVOLVING Account Type
> - Creditor Name equals consumer name (obvious mismatch)
> - Output contains narration outside TSV / QA FAIL block
> - Any fabricated placeholder values

> ```

> 
> Important guidelines for query formatting:
> - Your message must be a valid JSON object. Do not include any extraneous text, backticks, markdown formatting, or comments beyond what is shown.
> - The `queries` field must be a list of strings.
> - One of the queries **MUST** exactly match the user's original question, but stripped of any extraneous details, and with ambiguous references clearly resolved using context from the conversation. It **MUST** be a complete sentence.

>     - Unless the user's query is clearly unrelated to the uploaded files, prefer using `file_search.msearch`. You can always follow up with a web search if necessary for a more complete response.

> Content source: Source.file
> Title: MASTER_INDEX.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn1file1
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] MASTER INDEX — Credit Report TSV Extractor
> [L2] Spec Version: v1.4_LITE
> [L3] 
> [L4] MISSION
> [L5] Plug-and-play extraction of credit report PDFs into Google Sheets-ready TSV with minimal user input, maximum accuracy.
> [L6] 
> [L7] FILE PRECEDENCE
> [L8] SYSTEM_INSTRUCTIONS > MASTER_INDEX > SPEC_CORE > QA_AND_GATES > COMMON_MISTAKES
> [L9] 
> [L10] EXECUTION FLOW (NO NARRATION)
> [L11] 
> [L12] PASS 1 — EXTRACTION SNAPSHOT (INTERNAL)
> [L13] - Detect bureau (TU / EX / EQ)
> [L14] - Detect report generated date (as-of)
> [L15] - Identify tradeline sections
> [L16] - Count open tradelines
> [L17] - Count revolving vs loan tradelines
> [L18] - Detect unreadable pages or fragmented rows
> [L19] 
> [L20] If PASS 1 fails → Output QA FAIL — SNAPSHOT with exact missing items and STOP.
> [L21] 
> [L22] PASS 1.5 — EXTRACTION QUALITY GATE
> [L23] Run QA_AND_GATES → Extraction Quality Gate.
> [L24] If fails → Output QA FAIL — EXTRACTION QUALITY with exact reasons and STOP.
> [L25] 
> [L26] FAST-PATH RULE
> [L27] If PASS 1 and PASS 1.5 succeed with no ambiguity flags that are configured as hard-fail, proceed directly to PASS 2.
> [L28] 
> [L29] PASS 2 — FINAL OUTPUT
> [L30] A) Build structured tradeline table
> [L31] B) Apply schema/rules from SPEC_CORE
> [L32] C) Compute derived fields (age, utilization tiers)
> [L33] D) Sort oldest → newest
> [L34] E) Run FINAL QA (QA_AND_GATES)
> [L35]    - If fails → Output QA FAIL — FINAL QA with exact check(s) failed and STOP.
> [L36] F) Output TSV (silent mode)
> [L37] 
> [L38] QA FAIL OUTPUT FORMAT (MANDATORY)
> [L39] QA FAIL — <STAGE>
> [L40] Reason	Details
> [L41] <reason 1>	<details>
> [L42] <reason 2>	<details>

> [L1] Number of your accounts with a missed payment or derogatory indicator: 0 accounts
> [L2] About 99% of FICO High Achievers have no missed payments at all. But of those who do, the missed payment happened more than 4 years ago, on
> [L3] average.
> [L4] The FICO® Score evaluates if there are any missed payments being reported. Staying current and paying bills on time demonstrate lower credit risk.
> [L5]  Low revolving credit usage
> [L6] You've limited the use of your available revolving credit.
> [L7] Ratio of your revolving balances to your credit limits: 5%
> [L8] For FICO High Achievers, the average ratio of the revolving account balances to credit limits is 6%.
> [L9] FICO® Scores evaluate the total revolving account balances in relation to the total credit limits on those accounts. People who keep their ratio of
> [L10] balances to credit limits low pose less risk to lenders than those with higher ratios.
> [L11]  Long credit history
> [L12] You have an established credit history.
> [L13] Your oldest account was opened: 12 Years, 4 Months ago
> [L14] FICO High Achievers opened their oldest account 26 years ago, on average.
> [L15] Average age of your accounts: 4 Years, 3 Months
> [L16] Most FICO High Achievers have an average age of accounts of 10 years or more.
> [L17] FICO® Scores measure the age of the oldest account and the average age of all accounts being reported. Generally speaking, having a relatively long
> [L18] credit history and not opening many new accounts is regective of lower risk.
> [L19]  Recent credit card usage

> [L32] FICO® Scores evaluate the mix of credit cards, installment loans and mortgages. People who demonstrate recent and responsible use of credit cards
> [L33] and/or bank-issued open-ended accounts are generally considered less risky to lenders.

> Content source: Source.file
> Title: MASTER_INDEX.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn2file0
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] MASTER INDEX — Credit Report TSV Extractor
> [L2] Spec Version: v1.4_LITE
> [L3] 
> [L4] MISSION
> [L5] Plug-and-play extraction of credit report PDFs into Google Sheets-ready TSV with minimal user input, maximum accuracy.
> [L6] 
> [L7] FILE PRECEDENCE
> [L8] SYSTEM_INSTRUCTIONS > MASTER_INDEX > SPEC_CORE > QA_AND_GATES > COMMON_MISTAKES
> [L9] 
> [L10] EXECUTION FLOW (NO NARRATION)
> [L11] 
> [L12] PASS 1 — EXTRACTION SNAPSHOT (INTERNAL)
> [L13] - Detect bureau (TU / EX / EQ)
> [L14] - Detect report generated date (as-of)
> [L15] - Identify tradeline sections
> [L16] - Count open tradelines
> [L17] - Count revolving vs loan tradelines
> [L18] - Detect unreadable pages or fragmented rows
> [L19] 
> [L20] If PASS 1 fails → Output QA FAIL — SNAPSHOT with exact missing items and STOP.
> [L21] 
> [L22] PASS 1.5 — EXTRACTION QUALITY GATE
> [L23] Run QA_AND_GATES → Extraction Quality Gate.
> [L24] If fails → Output QA FAIL — EXTRACTION QUALITY with exact reasons and STOP.
> [L25] 
> [L26] FAST-PATH RULE
> [L27] If PASS 1 and PASS 1.5 succeed with no ambiguity flags that are configured as hard-fail, proceed directly to PASS 2.
> [L28] 
> [L29] PASS 2 — FINAL OUTPUT
> [L30] A) Build structured tradeline table
> [L31] B) Apply schema/rules from SPEC_CORE
> [L32] C) Compute derived fields (age, utilization tiers)
> [L33] D) Sort oldest → newest
> [L34] E) Run FINAL QA (QA_AND_GATES)
> [L35]    - If fails → Output QA FAIL — FINAL QA with exact check(s) failed and STOP.
> [L36] F) Output TSV (silent mode)
> [L37] 
> [L38] QA FAIL OUTPUT FORMAT (MANDATORY)
> [L39] QA FAIL — <STAGE>
> [L40] Reason	Details
> [L41] <reason 1>	<details>
> [L42] <reason 2>	<details>

> # 【0†MASTER_INDEX.txt†file-KQYJSDZuiZvumesfYK2Bhp】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: MASTER_INDEX.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn3file0
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] MASTER INDEX — Credit Report TSV Extractor
> [L2] Spec Version: v1.4_LITE
> [L3] 
> [L4] MISSION
> [L5] Plug-and-play extraction of credit report PDFs into Google Sheets-ready TSV with minimal user input, maximum accuracy.
> [L6] 
> [L7] FILE PRECEDENCE
> [L8] SYSTEM_INSTRUCTIONS > MASTER_INDEX > SPEC_CORE > QA_AND_GATES > COMMON_MISTAKES
> [L9] 
> [L10] EXECUTION FLOW (NO NARRATION)
> [L11] 
> [L12] PASS 1 — EXTRACTION SNAPSHOT (INTERNAL)
> [L13] - Detect bureau (TU / EX / EQ)
> [L14] - Detect report generated date (as-of)
> [L15] - Identify tradeline sections
> [L16] - Count open tradelines
> [L17] - Count revolving vs loan tradelines
> [L18] - Detect unreadable pages or fragmented rows
> [L19] 
> [L20] If PASS 1 fails → Output QA FAIL — SNAPSHOT with exact missing items and STOP.
> [L21] 
> [L22] PASS 1.5 — EXTRACTION QUALITY GATE
> [L23] Run QA_AND_GATES → Extraction Quality Gate.
> [L24] If fails → Output QA FAIL — EXTRACTION QUALITY with exact reasons and STOP.
> [L25] 
> [L26] FAST-PATH RULE
> [L27] If PASS 1 and PASS 1.5 succeed with no ambiguity flags that are configured as hard-fail, proceed directly to PASS 2.
> [L28] 
> [L29] PASS 2 — FINAL OUTPUT
> [L30] A) Build structured tradeline table
> [L31] B) Apply schema/rules from SPEC_CORE
> [L32] C) Compute derived fields (age, utilization tiers)
> [L33] D) Sort oldest → newest
> [L34] E) Run FINAL QA (QA_AND_GATES)
> [L35]    - If fails → Output QA FAIL — FINAL QA with exact check(s) failed and STOP.
> [L36] F) Output TSV (silent mode)
> [L37] 
> [L38] QA FAIL OUTPUT FORMAT (MANDATORY)
> [L39] QA FAIL — <STAGE>
> [L40] Reason	Details
> [L41] <reason 1>	<details>
> [L42] <reason 2>	<details>

> # 【1†MASTER_INDEX.txt†file-KQYJSDZuiZvumesfYK2Bhp】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: MASTER_INDEX.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn4file1
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] MASTER INDEX — Credit Report TSV Extractor
> [L2] Spec Version: v1.4_LITE
> [L3] 
> [L4] MISSION
> [L5] Plug-and-play extraction of credit report PDFs into Google Sheets-ready TSV with minimal user input, maximum accuracy.
> [L6] 
> [L7] FILE PRECEDENCE
> [L8] SYSTEM_INSTRUCTIONS > MASTER_INDEX > SPEC_CORE > QA_AND_GATES > COMMON_MISTAKES
> [L9] 
> [L10] EXECUTION FLOW (NO NARRATION)
> [L11] 
> [L12] PASS 1 — EXTRACTION SNAPSHOT (INTERNAL)
> [L13] - Detect bureau (TU / EX / EQ)
> [L14] - Detect report generated date (as-of)
> [L15] - Identify tradeline sections
> [L16] - Count open tradelines
> [L17] - Count revolving vs loan tradelines
> [L18] - Detect unreadable pages or fragmented rows
> [L19] 
> [L20] If PASS 1 fails → Output QA FAIL — SNAPSHOT with exact missing items and STOP.
> [L21] 
> [L22] PASS 1.5 — EXTRACTION QUALITY GATE
> [L23] Run QA_AND_GATES → Extraction Quality Gate.
> [L24] If fails → Output QA FAIL — EXTRACTION QUALITY with exact reasons and STOP.
> [L25] 
> [L26] FAST-PATH RULE
> [L27] If PASS 1 and PASS 1.5 succeed with no ambiguity flags that are configured as hard-fail, proceed directly to PASS 2.
> [L28] 
> [L29] PASS 2 — FINAL OUTPUT
> [L30] A) Build structured tradeline table
> [L31] B) Apply schema/rules from SPEC_CORE
> [L32] C) Compute derived fields (age, utilization tiers)
> [L33] D) Sort oldest → newest
> [L34] E) Run FINAL QA (QA_AND_GATES)
> [L35]    - If fails → Output QA FAIL — FINAL QA with exact check(s) failed and STOP.
> [L36] F) Output TSV (silent mode)
> [L37] 
> [L38] QA FAIL OUTPUT FORMAT (MANDATORY)
> [L39] QA FAIL — <STAGE>
> [L40] Reason	Details
> [L41] <reason 1>	<details>
> [L42] <reason 2>	<details>

> [L4] You've shown recent use of credit cards and/or bank-issued open-ended accounts.
> [L5] FICO® Scores evaluate the mix of credit cards, installment loans and mortgages. People who demonstrate recent and responsible use of credit cards
> [L6] and/or bank-issued open-ended accounts are generally considered less risky to lenders.
> [L7] What's hurting
> [L8]  Short revolving history
> [L9] You have not established a long revolving and/or open-ended account credit history.
> [L10] Your ,rst revolving and/or open-ended account was opened: 6 Years, 11 Months ago
> [L11] FICO High Achievers opened their ,rst revolving and/or open-ended account 25 years ago, on average.
> [L12] People with longer credit histories who infrequently open new accounts generally pose less risk to lenders. In your case, the age of your oldest revolving
> [L13] and/or open-ended account and/or the average age of your revolving and/or open-ended accounts is relatively low. <PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 53 / 53> Experian 12/17/25, 3:06 PM
> [L14] https://usa.experian.com/mfe/credit/printable-report/transunion/202512172303312920 Page 53 of 53
> [L15] Prepared For ALAN NGUYEN Date generated: Dec 17, 2025
> [L16] Disclaimer
> [L17] About your FICO Score 8 or other FICO Scores
> [L18] Your FICO Score 8 powered by Experian data is formulated using the information in your credit ,le at the time it is requested. Many but not all
> [L19] lenders use FICO Score 8. In addition to the FICO Score 8, we may offer and provide other base or industry-speci,c FICO Scores (such as
> [L20] FICO Auto Scores and FICO Bankcard Scores). The other FICO Scores made available are calculated from versions of the base and
> [L21] industry-speci,c FICO Score models.
> [L22] Base FICO Scores (including the FICO Score 8) range from 300 to 850. Industry-speci,c FICO Scores range from 250-900. Higher scores
> [L23] represent a greater likelihood that you'll pay back your debts so you are viewed as being a lower credit risk to lenders. A lower FICO Score
> [L24] indicates to lenders that you may be a higher credit risk. There are many scoring models used in the marketplace. The type of score used, and
> [L25] its associated risk levels, may vary from lender to lender. But regardless of what scoring model is used, they all have one purpose: to
> [L26] summarize your creditworthiness. Keep in mind that your score is just one factor used in the application process. Other factors, such as your
> [L27] annual salary and length of employment, may also be considered by lenders when you apply for a loan.
> [L28] What this means to you:
> [L29] Credit scoring can help you understand your overall credit rating and help companies better understand how to serve you. Overall bene,ts of
> [L30] credit scoring have included faster credit approvals, reduction in human error and bias, consistency, and better terms and rates for American
> [L31] consumers through reduced costs and losses for lenders. Your lender or insurer may use a different FICO Score than FICO Score 8 or other
> [L32] base or industry-speci,c FICO Scores provided by us, or different scoring models to determine how you score.

> Content Snippet:
> [L1] Number of your accounts with a missed payment or derogatory indicator: 0 accounts
> [L2] About 99% of FICO High Achievers have no missed payments at all. But of those who do, the missed payment happened more than 4 years ago, on
> [L3] average.
> [L4] The FICO® Score evaluates if there are any missed payments being reported. Staying current and paying bills on time demonstrate lower credit risk.
> [L5]  Low revolving credit usage
> [L6] You've limited the use of your available revolving credit.
> [L7] Ratio of your revolving balances to your credit limits: 5%
> [L8] For FICO High Achievers, the average ratio of the revolving account balances to credit limits is 6%.
> [L9] FICO® Scores evaluate the total revolving account balances in relation to the total credit limits on those accounts. People who keep their ratio of
> [L10] balances to credit limits low pose less risk to lenders than those with higher ratios.
> [L11]  Long credit history
> [L12] You have an established credit history.
> [L13] Your oldest account was opened: 12 Years, 4 Months ago
> [L14] FICO High Achievers opened their oldest account 26 years ago, on average.
> [L15] Average age of your accounts: 4 Years, 3 Months
> [L16] Most FICO High Achievers have an average age of accounts of 10 years or more.
> [L17] FICO® Scores measure the age of the oldest account and the average age of all accounts being reported. Generally speaking, having a relatively long
> [L18] credit history and not opening many new accounts is regective of lower risk.
> [L19]  Recent credit card usage
> [L20] ®
> [L21] 300 850
> [L22] Poor
> [L23] Fair
> [L24] Good Very Good
> [L25] 740 - 799 Exceptional

> [L31] You've shown recent use of credit cards and/or bank-issued open-ended accounts.
> [L32] FICO® Scores evaluate the mix of credit cards, installment loans and mortgages. People who demonstrate recent and responsible use of credit cards
> [L33] and/or bank-issued open-ended accounts are generally considered less risky to lenders.
> [L34] What's hurting
> [L35]  Short revolving history
> [L36] You have not established a long revolving and/or open-ended account credit history.
> [L37] Your ,rst revolving and/or open-ended account was opened: 6 Years, 11 Months ago
> [L38] FICO High Achievers opened their ,rst revolving and/or open-ended account 25 years ago, on average.
> [L39] People with longer credit histories who infrequently open new accounts generally pose less risk to lenders. In your case, the age of your oldest revolving

> # 【1†MASTER_INDEX.txt†file-KQYJSDZuiZvumesfYK2Bhp】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: MASTER_INDEX.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn5file1
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] MASTER INDEX — Credit Report TSV Extractor
> [L2] Spec Version: v1.4_LITE
> [L3] 
> [L4] MISSION
> [L5] Plug-and-play extraction of credit report PDFs into Google Sheets-ready TSV with minimal user input, maximum accuracy.
> [L6] 
> [L7] FILE PRECEDENCE
> [L8] SYSTEM_INSTRUCTIONS > MASTER_INDEX > SPEC_CORE > QA_AND_GATES > COMMON_MISTAKES
> [L9] 
> [L10] EXECUTION FLOW (NO NARRATION)
> [L11] 
> [L12] PASS 1 — EXTRACTION SNAPSHOT (INTERNAL)
> [L13] - Detect bureau (TU / EX / EQ)
> [L14] - Detect report generated date (as-of)
> [L15] - Identify tradeline sections
> [L16] - Count open tradelines
> [L17] - Count revolving vs loan tradelines
> [L18] - Detect unreadable pages or fragmented rows
> [L19] 
> [L20] If PASS 1 fails → Output QA FAIL — SNAPSHOT with exact missing items and STOP.
> [L21] 
> [L22] PASS 1.5 — EXTRACTION QUALITY GATE
> [L23] Run QA_AND_GATES → Extraction Quality Gate.
> [L24] If fails → Output QA FAIL — EXTRACTION QUALITY with exact reasons and STOP.
> [L25] 
> [L26] FAST-PATH RULE
> [L27] If PASS 1 and PASS 1.5 succeed with no ambiguity flags that are configured as hard-fail, proceed directly to PASS 2.
> [L28] 
> [L29] PASS 2 — FINAL OUTPUT
> [L30] A) Build structured tradeline table
> [L31] B) Apply schema/rules from SPEC_CORE
> [L32] C) Compute derived fields (age, utilization tiers)
> [L33] D) Sort oldest → newest
> [L34] E) Run FINAL QA (QA_AND_GATES)
> [L35]    - If fails → Output QA FAIL — FINAL QA with exact check(s) failed and STOP.
> [L36] F) Output TSV (silent mode)
> [L37] 
> [L38] QA FAIL OUTPUT FORMAT (MANDATORY)
> [L39] QA FAIL — <STAGE>
> [L40] Reason	Details
> [L41] <reason 1>	<details>
> [L42] <reason 2>	<details>

> [L16] Disclaimer
> [L17] About your FICO Score 8 or other FICO Scores
> [L18] Your FICO Score 8 powered by Experian data is formulated using the information in your credit ,le at the time it is requested. Many but not all
> [L19] lenders use FICO Score 8. In addition to the FICO Score 8, we may offer and provide other base or industry-speci,c FICO Scores (such as
> [L20] FICO Auto Scores and FICO Bankcard Scores). The other FICO Scores made available are calculated from versions of the base and
> [L21] industry-speci,c FICO Score models.
> [L22] Base FICO Scores (including the FICO Score 8) range from 300 to 850. Industry-speci,c FICO Scores range from 250-900. Higher scores
> [L23] represent a greater likelihood that you'll pay back your debts so you are viewed as being a lower credit risk to lenders. A lower FICO Score
> [L24] indicates to lenders that you may be a higher credit risk. There are many scoring models used in the marketplace. The type of score used, and
> [L25] its associated risk levels, may vary from lender to lender. But regardless of what scoring model is used, they all have one purpose: to
> [L26] summarize your creditworthiness. Keep in mind that your score is just one factor used in the application process. Other factors, such as your
> [L27] annual salary and length of employment, may also be considered by lenders when you apply for a loan.
> [L28] What this means to you:
> [L29] Credit scoring can help you understand your overall credit rating and help companies better understand how to serve you. Overall bene,ts of
> [L30] credit scoring have included faster credit approvals, reduction in human error and bias, consistency, and better terms and rates for American
> [L31] consumers through reduced costs and losses for lenders. Your lender or insurer may use a different FICO Score than FICO Score 8 or other
> [L32] base or industry-speci,c FICO Scores provided by us, or different scoring models to determine how you score.
